Since 2001, the tender for the organisation of days and auctions has not been concluded, and the contract has been annexed without documented attempts of market discernment among other companies involved in the organisation of large events. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u13	You're welcome, Minister, to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u14	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Mr. Speaker! First of all, it should be pointed out that the metropolitan union law contains delegations for a number of implementing acts, in fact, three implementing acts. Firstly, regulations setting the boundaries of individual metropolitan areas for the creation of metropolitan unions by identifying municipalities that are part of metropolitan areas, i.e. the so-called metropolitan areas. a delimitation regulation, followed by a regulation setting out the procedure for the submission of an application by a municipality council within the limits of metropolitan areas and the scope of the documents to be attached to the application, as you mentioned, and a third regulation which is already finalising, creating metropolitan unions in the various metropolitan areas, at the same time establishing their names. In this context, I must point out that the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration has drawn up a draft regulation of the Council of Ministers on the procedure for submitting proposals for the creation of metropolitan unions and the documents required in these matters, as authorised by the mandate, as mentioned by the Member, in Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 3 metropolitan union laws. However, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to clarify that, in order for this regulation to be applicable, it is still necessary to issue this earlier delimitation regulation, which sets out precisely the areas that will potentially form part of the metropolis, which is, as I have already mentioned, the so-called. a delimitation regulation. The areas of municipalities and cities which will be able to request that they be covered by this area should be identified in the first place. It is worth noting that the Minister for Development, with whom we cooperate on the issue of that regulation, is unfortunately also a key role in the area delimitation. I would also like to inform you that, as part of the ongoing cooperation with the Ministry of Development, the difficulties concerning the delimitation have also been signalled. Difficulties arise, among others. the fact that work should be started in the near future on the revision of existing land use documents, i.e. the concept of land use in 2030. This obligation arises from the Act, and by 2018, this imposes a law on us on spatial planning and development and we need to analyse possible changes. High Izbo, I would also like to point out that defining the boundaries of metropolitan areas and then establishing metropolitan unions is important for the sustainable development of the country. A link with specific policy-making tasks for transport and spatial development and with additional resources from the state budget and from the budgets of the municipalities forming it is an institutional tool for the development of the metropolitan area. It is therefore particularly important to define its boundaries wisely. Concentration on municipalities forming the so-called. The core of the metropolitan area and the omission of certain local authorities from the area, you can say, extreme can lead to disparities in their development. For this reason, this issue requires a specific diagnosis of the functional and development processes that occur in these areas. High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, It should also be noted that the time limits laid down in the Act for the establishment of metropolitan unions at the moment do not seem realistic enough to be fulfilled, since, according to the Act, a regulation should already be issued by the end of April for the establishment of a specific metropolitan area. And a large number of comments that come from both local authorities and, so to speak, from experts, indicate that this will have to be slightly modified. Modifications will be required to some extent by law. In the context of my information, it is important that the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration is currently working on an in-depth analysis of this problem. In the near future, we will probably want to propose a change in the proposed solutions. Nevertheless, in the context which is falling here, in the context of Silesia, the voivodships, i.e. the Silesian Voivodeship, are currently cooperating with representatives of local governments, they are kept informed of their intentions and plans in terms of possible changes to the law and the introduction of more precise provisions that will allow for a more realistic implementation of its provisions. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u24	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I answer the questions of Mr Tomasz Romankowski. As far as the question of the stability of the banking sector is concerned, it is so that I can say with some determination that there is no risk associated with the bankruptcy of banks in Poland. The fact is that the whole banking sector is a regulated market, therefore, it can be said that there is quite effective supervision - of course, with certain doubts about the functioning of KNF in the past, but, you can say, it is that this macro-prudential supervision functions relatively effectively and there are no dangers on the horizon that could allow us to put such risks also that there is a risk of bankruptcy of any bank. These surveillance instruments work well. First of all, I would like to remind you that there is a macro-prudential policy in Poland, there is a Financial Stability Committee - it is the body responsible for macro-prudential supervision of the financial system - there are measures to strengthen financial stability. Supervision shall be directed towards combating the creation and reduction of systemic risk which could lead to the destabilisation of the financial system. The Chairman of the Financial Stability Committee is the President of the National Bank of Poland, the Committee consists of the Minister of Finance, the Chairman of the Financial Supervision Commission and the President of the Management Board of the Banking Guarantee Fund. This is a body in which these four main institutions responsible for the entire financial sector in Poland, of course, cooperate closely with each other. Two meetings of the Financial Stability Committee have already been held, chaired by the President of the NBP, on 27 December Ub. on 22 February and 22 February. Each meeting was followed by a communication containing information on the topics discussed at the meeting as well as on the decisions taken. In addition, which is worth highlighting, e.g. the latest report on financial stability, which was published in February - this is the report of the National Bank of Poland - also points to the stable situation of the banking sector in Poland. The information we can find in this report shows that this sector is well equipped with capital. For example, the Tier 1 base ratio increased between January and September last year from 14% to 14,8%. The satisfactory capital position also confirms the small share of banks with capital adequacy ratios below, you can say, this benchmark. Therefore, there are no real signs of significant risks. The report on the stability of the financial system also draws attention to the healthy nature of the banking sector, which is the increase in loans to the non-financial sector at a pace similar to the nominal rate of GDP growth. So we don't really have an extraordinary, excessive, or too slow, rate of credit growth for the non-financial sector. In addition, at the end, what I think is important to me is that, in the context of financial stability, it is worth paying attention to the work on the implementation of two important directives - here, in Parliament - the BRR and DGS directives, whose implementation is based on the draft law on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the deposit guarantee system and the forced restructuring. This is a project that's being processed now. And these two directives are important because the aim of the BRR directive is to establish a system in which the involvement of taxpayers, i.e. from the state budget, is limited, in the situation of rescuing the banking sector. In the case of problems in the banking sector, the losses are to be borne first by creditors, the bank's shareholders, which will also reduce such an excessive risk tendency. [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the banking sector. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u31	I ask the Minister to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u32	Minister! I'm sorry. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mrs. Member! The position of certain judges of the Constitutional Tribunal presented on 9 March 2016 only creates an external appearance of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal and is not a judgment on the legal side. It should be noted that although the case concerning the non-compliance with the Constitution of the Law of 22 December 2015 on the amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal Act should be dealt with in full, three judges elected by the Polish Parliament of the Constitutional Tribunal, from whom the President of the Republic of Poland took the oath, were not allowed to consider it. The omission of those judges caused the case not to be dealt with by the Constitutional Tribunal as the body appointed to rule on the compatibility of normative acts with the Constitution. The decision of such a body does not therefore come from an authorized constitutional body and, what is important to us, does not contain all the signatures of the judges, and this is a sine qua non condition of the judgment. Thus, such a position cannot exist in legal proceedings as a judgment. [[Voice from the courtroom: Judgment.]] The standard resulting directly from the article cannot be disregarded. Paragraph 194. 1 the Constitution according to which the Constitutional Tribunal consists of 15 judges and so many judges, as indicated above, have been properly elected. Therefore, by refusing to allow judges of the Constitutional Tribunal to rule in the present case, the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, in our view, infringed the Article. 7 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In addition, the present case is referred to as the case. Act K 47/15 should be dealt with in accordance with the provisions of the Act of 25 June 2015 on the Constitutional Court in the current version of the Act of 22 December 2015 amending the Act on the Constitutional Court, since each law benefits from the presumption of constitutionality and as such should be applied by the tribunal and your President. Accordingly, any public authority, including the Constitutional Tribunal, should respect the applicable provisions which are making use of the presumption of constitutionality. The Court's arbitrary recognition by the Constitutional Tribunal that the Law of 22 December 2015 amending the Constitutional Tribunal Act does not benefit from the presumption of constitutionality and cannot apply in the proceedings being conducted in that case, infringes the principle of legalism as set out in Article. 7 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. As a result, the position given on 9 March 2016 wrongly referred to as the judgment merely expresses the views of certain judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. [[Really?]] The Constitutional Tribunal in our assessment of its actions in the present case may have infringed the Constitution's provisions, including the Article. 7, expressing the principle which I mentioned above and which is contained in Article. 197, which provides that the organisation of the Constitutional Tribunal and the procedure for proceedings before the Tribunal shall be governed by a law. The non-application of the Law of 25 June 2015 on the Constitutional Tribunal in its existing version, as amended by the Act of 22 December 2015 amending the Act on the Constitutional Court, therefore constitutes an infringement of Article. 197 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In view of the circumstances presented by me, it should be noted that there is no judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal on the compatibility with the Constitution of the Law of 22 December 2015, which could be published in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland. It should be stressed that the President of the Council of Ministers, as a constitutional body, is required to comply with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, and in particular with Article. 7 principles of legalism according to which public authorities operate on the basis and within the limits of the law. The position of certain judges of the Constitutional Tribunal of 9 March would therefore constitute an infringement of the constitutional principle of legalism and of Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the application of this Regulation. 2 of the Constitution, according to which judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal are subject to immediate publication in the Official Journal. So, in fact, the legal changes are of course important here and will certainly be introduced, but the main force of action... On the part of the state administration, this should be primarily a matter of practical action. In this sense, indeed, the VAT gap is a problem of inefficient action, and it can be said that the limited efficiency of the tax administration's activities is limited. Therefore, the plans presented by Minister Shamacha are based on a three-piece programme. Firstly, this is the provision to the tax authorities of appropriate IT tools that provide information on potential fraud in real time. Here is really the project, the core of which is the central invoice register, i.e. the invoices which are later the basis for extortion, would be available in real time and this is the basis for having confidence and hope that this instrument works. I'll talk about international experiences later. Secondly, a project is being prepared on the national tax administration, i.e. linking this part of the tax administration to the customs administration on the one hand. Of course, there will also be the part of the administration responsible for tax control. Because at the same time we have such a diagnosis, and this is a common diagnosis, that the important problem of these frauds is the limited efficiency of cooperation between customs and fiscal structures. This synergy is necessary in order to actually counteract VAT fraud. Why do we have reason to believe that this system can work? Firstly, there is a Portuguese effect in the literature analysing the functioning of these solutions. The information that Portugal is working on the preparation of such an IT solution has reduced the VAT gap by 30% over the year. 30% of 40 billion is 12 billion PLN, it is worth remembering that. Secondly, there is an example of the state of Ohio, whose tax office collects 21 types of taxes. The IT system operating there allowed to increase revenue levels to 2% on an annual basis. And Russia, which has implemented this system, has increased its VAT tax revenue by 20% as a result of the introduction of this IT solution in recent years. So we're 20%. greater VAT revenue. In addition, such solutions, very similar, function in Slovakia. Since 2012, the result is that this level of VAT deficiency is decreasing annually at such a rate that if we actually assumed that at the same rate this level of VAT deficiency, and in Poland it is at 25% A little more than 25%... If this Slovak effect applied to Poland on the same scale, it would mean raising VAT revenues, based on an increase in the tax base, by about 10 million a year. In addition, it is worth referring to the Dutch experience. So that it is not a new thing, it is worth mentioning. There are a lot of positive effects from all over the world. In fact, we estimate that the effects will occur at the moment that this company, which is going to build this tool, will end up and this system will start working. In fact, 2016 is the period during which these solutions will be implemented. We assume that in 2017 really these real, already important fiscal effects will occur. As far as access to trade secrets is concerned, we assume that this company will indeed prepare such IT solutions that it will later practice the use of these IT solutions within public institutions. And of course, because we're dealing with two very sensitive elements, i.e. first of all, with access to data, for example on a non-nomised and test level, and second of all, access to source codes that can be critical, [[♪ The bell ♪]] We assume that this will also be controlled by state services. Thank you very much.

I will refer to the draft Posting of Workers Act. The main features of this implementation will consist of: defining the conditions of employment of posted workers in our country for the regulation in the Labour Code; defining rules for administrative cooperation; defining rules for access to information on posting of workers in our country; regulating the employer's submission of a declaration of intention to posting; introducing a criterion to assess whether the rules for posting of workers have been correct; regulating the issue of joint and several liability in the construction of the contractor ordering the performance of the service and the employer posting workers in our country; introducing rules enabling cross-border enforcement of administrative penalties and fines imposed on entrepreneurs posting workers from our country and imposing sanctions for violation of the law. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u104	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Seym! The fundamental amendments to the Code of Criminal Procedure, which entered into force on 1 July 2015, were referred to as the reform of the so-called criminal procedure. the inter-disciplinary model of the criminal trial. Its purpose was to create the right conditions to make factual findings on the basis of the outcome of the dispute brought before the parties before the court, and in fact it caused the application of the overriding principle of factually correct findings and the actual location in a more favourable procedural situation of wealthier or more operational individuals. The current model of the process overrides the principle of adversariality in relation to the principle of material truth, while marginalising the active role of the court, which, due to its impartiality, can and should, in such proceedings, act as a guarantor both of the individual's powers and of the effectiveness of the conduct in terms of achieving its objectives, and in particular of the appropriate criminal response, the High Sejm. The formal obligation to seek the truth does not correspond to the right of the court to allow and carry out evidence of its own motion only in exceptional cases justified by special circumstances. Moreover, the courts can demonstrate a variety of evidence activities, which, in the light of the statutory ban on the imposition of pleas in law concerning the initiative of evidence of a court acting ex officio, adds to the possibility of unequal unfair treatment of the parties by different courts in the same situations. Indeed, it is completely in line with the applicable regulations both to demonstrate absolute passiveness by the formation of the court as regards the evidence initiative and to carry out all evidence ex officio by the court without looking at the evidence requests submitted by the parties. The full adversariality introduced by the latest novel leads to situations in which the court decides only on the basis of the evidence submitted by the parties, and does not seek to establish the true state of affairs. This results in the primacy of the principle of judicial truth, i.e. the party that will convince the court to its own merits, regardless of what it looks like, will win the trial. There is also no need for great insight to determine who will be better found in the thicket of regulations and evidence. Of course, those who are better placed, the High Sejm, which will additionally afford to hire the best defenders and conduct a costly process. However, this does not have much to do with the principle of truth, equality and equal treatment of all defendants. According to the applicable rules, the prosecutor must not only conduct or supervise the investigation, but also carry out all the evidence in these cases before the court, i.e. Interrogate witnesses, read documents. It should be borne in mind that the system introduced poses a great challenge for the organisation of the prosecutor's office and may even paralyze its work. It is not only that the level of the prosecutor's procedural responsibilities has been significantly increased while maintaining the organisation of the institution, but also that the prosecution's cases go to different courts in different formations, which in fact makes it impossible for him to participate in the judicial stage in the cases he is conducting. The consequence of such defective establishment of the procedural activity of the participants in the proceedings - setting, I apologize - the consequence of such defective setting of the procedural activity of the participants in the proceedings is a much greater risk of issuing an unfair judgment acquittal by the court, i.e. to the person who actually committed the crime. Any shortcomings, even if purely formal, on the part of the prosecutor will result in a failure by him to prove his cause and thus release from criminal liability, the High Sejm. In view of the short period since the entry into force of the amendment on 1 July 2015, there is no statistical data on this subject, but the public statements of the members of the previous codification committee, which estimated the rate of acquittal in cases recognised in the adversarial model at issue at around 30%, while the inquisition model did not exceed 2%. The decisive primacy of the principle of adversariality in the proceedings in the courts has led the proceedings to be used, in accordance with the current wording of the law, only to obtain evidence to the extent necessary to establish the merits of bringing an indictment or other termination of proceedings, meaning that the obligation to clarify the case in a comprehensive manner at this stage is waived. Such a role of the preparatory procedure in the criminal trial system has led to the introduction by the legislator of detailed formal requirements which must be met by prosecutions and other prosecutors' complaints. Dividing evidence into groups, describing every evidence in the indictment, rigorous interpretation by the courts of conditions that must meet the thesis of evidence, makes it more frequent for the courts to decide to return prosecutions to the prosecutor. Those orders are not subject to judicial review by a higher court in accordance with the general principle, but are subject only to judicial review by a court competent to settle the case, i.e. in which the Judge making the return is held. According to incomplete data from the Public Prosecutor's Office for 2015 due to formal deficiencies, prosecutors - and here's a remark, High Izbo - about 3,000. cases. Finally, the assessment of the existing regulations on so-called changes cannot be overlooked. Consensual termination of criminal proceedings. It can be said without exaggeration that the authors of the consensus novel have made the procedure based on procedural agreements an alternative way of dealing with, sometimes very serious, criminal cases. It is not only that they have allowed such an opportunity for all without exception the crimes provided for in the Penal Code, including the most serious crimes, that I will mention here murders, rapes with particular cruelty, but that is why they have introduced a stand-alone condition for extraordinary relief of punishment. Consequently, in the event of such a request being taken into account, the court shall not carry out any evidence proceedings and may, in respect of offences, deal with the whole matter at the hearing. On the other hand, it is a stand-alone condition for an extraordinary relief of punishment, e.g. The mere fact that the perpetrator of a murder committed for a minimum of eight years imprisonment of between 1 month and 7 years and 11 months is that he has applied for punishment without taking evidence. There can't be an agreement to this kind of thing. As a result, in the second half of 2015, approximately 1/3 of the cases were concluded in the preparatory proceedings for the application of a constitutional penalty, while the year before that was only 15%. In turn in absolute numbers, i.e. Despite the absence of significant changes in the level of influence of prosecution cases, there was a marked decrease in the number of indictments brought before the court: in the second half of 2015, it was 74 000. cases, and the year before - up to 115 thousand. matters, High Sejm. Similarly, it should be pointed out that the Article is incomprehensible from the point of view of the principles of a fair criminal response. 59a of the Penal Code. In fact, it provides for compulsory remission of criminal proceedings against the perpetrators of certain offences at the request of the victim. There is no need for great imagination to say that the mechanism adopted is not enough to ensure that the perpetrators of many cases are not punished, it still leads to the possibility of many abuses related to the unauthorised influence of perpetrators or criminal groups on the victims in order to induce them to make such requests. The negative, harmful to public order and order, the fight against crime and the security of the public, the consequences of the entry into force of the so-called. Therefore, the adversarial novel to the Code of Criminal Procedure must be reversed as soon as possible. The government has therefore taken a legislative initiative in this area and today the High House will proceed with an appropriate amendment to restore the previously existing inquisition process model to ensure that the findings of the procedural authorities are consistent with the material truth, of course with the active role of the court, which is of great importance in terms of the results of the process, which has clearly shown these statistics. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u135	Thank you very much. And to speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I ask Mr Arkadiusz Myrcha. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u136	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In spring 2013, a draft of one of the largest reforms of the Code of Criminal Procedure was launched in the Sejm for 6 years. After 6 months of parliamentary work, including the operation of a special emergency committee, a model was developed not only to meet the current challenges of criminal proceedings, but also to eliminate many of the procedures that have been growing over the years to prevent a smooth investigation of the case. One of the main elements of this major reform was the introduction of so strongly criticised by the authors of the so-called law. rules of adversariality. Its purpose was to change the procedural role of the court from the investigating body, which conducts the investigation, to the role of the arbitration panel, which settles between the parties at issue, which are the prosecutor and the accused in the criminal proceedings. This amendment was a response to the shortcomings of the previous model of criminal proceedings, namely the duplication of investigations. On the one hand, the investigation, which was conducted by the prosecution or the police, sought to clarify the circumstances of the case as comprehensively as possible and to safeguard the evidence for the court, and on the other hand, the entire investigation of evidence was repeated in the course of legal proceedings. This evidently resulted in an unnecessary extension of the entire procedure on several occasions. Several months of operation of the new system showed where adjustments should be made. With such an extensive change, mistakes are inevitable, which are only visible at the stage of application of the law. However, the direction conferred by the 2013 Act was right and fully justified. Therefore, it cannot be agreed with the allegations made by the authors of the draft law that the introduction of the principle of adversariality constitutes an obstacle to the investigation of the truth in court. This is evident and unfortunately intentional abuse. The fundamental principle of material truth, or decision on the basis of actual facts, is found in the current Code of Criminal Procedure. The proposal to return to the so-called model should therefore be negatively assessed. Inquisition proceedings in which the court takes full responsibility for the outcome of the process and thus becomes an active participant in the proceedings. With this principle, it corresponds to the new wording of the article proposed in the draft law. 167, namely the introduction of an unlimited initiative of evidence of the court. It should therefore be recognised that the Law and Justice propose a return to a solution which could result in a breach of the principle of impartiality of the court. It is also negative to assess the proposal for a new content of the Article. 168a and Art. 168b Code of Criminal Procedure, which introduces the possibility of carrying out and using evidence obtained for the purpose of criminal proceedings by means of a criminal offence, because these provisions correlate with the highly controversial law on the prosecution recently adopted by the Supreme Chamber. In the present form, they provide a certain assurance that no illegal act, no illegal wiretap or provocation can prove to anyone that they are being accused. The sad experiences in 2005 and 2007 show that such a provision is necessary. Unfortunately, the Law and Justice which entrusted the supervision of special services to Mariusz Kaminski, and the administration of the prosecutor's office to Minister Ziobra, felt that this fuse might be difficult for them to implement criminal policy. Therefore, it is impossible not to recall the two recently adopted laws, which are extremely important from the point of view of this project. It is about the Police Act, i.e. the so-called. Surveillance, and the D.A.'s Attorney's Law Act. Thus, removing illegal evidence from the code creates a picture of a reality in which few people want to find themselves. A negative assessment should be made of the proposal for changes in the award of the defender ex officio as described in Article. 78 and Article 7 (1) (a) (ii) and (iii) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council 80a Code of Criminal Procedure. Well, the Law and Justice Reform provided the possibility of obtaining a defender for a suspect, that is, already at the stage of police or prosecutorial proceedings. This is extremely important, because that's when the first hearings, the examination of evidence and the imposition of charges occur. According to the proposed bill, this entitlement is abolished. Thus, only the person who can afford to pay a professional lawyer will be able to count on the lawyer. Free assistance will only be available at the stage of legal proceedings, i.e. after the indictment has been prepared. It's a clear hit on the citizen's right to a fair defense. It is also critical to refer to the proposal to delete the article. 59a of the Penal Code, whose application is positively assessed. It gives the possibility to cancel the proceedings even before the start of the court case and, what is important, only at the request of the victim. Making the ideology that this provision serves the rich to buy their own innocence is, in principle, dishonest and puts this necessary and useful institution in a false light. This is a particular area of functioning of a state that clearly requires peace, reason and evolutionary change. Any other way will cause the most suffering to those whose courts are to defend, i.e. Polish citizens. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club shall amend the report presented. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Thanks to these characteristics of the law, the entity has the possibility to decide on its conduct based on full knowledge of the conditions of the functioning of the state authorities and the legal consequences that its actions may entail. An entity should be able to determine both the consequences of individual behaviours and events on the basis of the legal situation in force at a given point in time, and to expect that the legislator will not change them arbitraryly. ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u174	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! We will also all agree that electronic surveillance is a kind of victorious compromise between the law enforcement apparatus of the state and the convicted. On the one hand, the electronic surveillance system is very positive in the rehabilitation process. Thanks to such a system of punishment, the sentenced have the opportunity to work, continue learning, and maintain and maintain family ties. There are no negative psychological and social consequences associated with detention in the surveillance system. On the other hand, the electronic surveillance system is a huge savings for the State Treasury. The statistics clearly show that, in the case of imprisonment, the percentage of recidivism is extremely high. The intention to introduce the current wording of the rules on the electronic surveillance system was to restrict the use of imprisonment so that the punished do not commit crimes in the future. It is well known that much better rehabilitation effects occur when reacting with the penalty of freedom. This punishment may naturally be distressing, but it does not bring with it negative elements that are linked to the stay in a prison. The application of the sentence of the restriction of liberty, and thus of supervision, was intended to facilitate a significant reduction in the use of the sentence of imprisonment with conditional suspension of its execution, as well as the possibility of imposing a sentence of limitation of liberty in the event of a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding 8 years. The consequence of the reform of July ub. r. was also the transfer of the power to apply electronic supervision from the penitentiary courts to the courts ruling on the case and, in a broader perspective, the moment when it was decided to apply supervision from the execution stage at the time of the sentence. The July reform was not written on the knee, day by day. Multi-monthly work on the amendment has made sure that the proposed solutions will have a positive effect. However, by referring to the justification for the draft law, the claim of the designers that the direct cause of such a state of affairs is the assignment of electronic supervision in the July 2015 novel to the penalty of the restriction of freedom is unjustified. In the present case, the government does not seem to note that in addition to the changes in the electronic surveillance system in July ub. The greatest reform of criminal law in the history of the Third Republic took place. A number of factors should be taken into account when examining the reasons for the decrease in the number of convicted offenders in the electronic surveillance system, such as the ratio of the penalties imposed and the restrictions on freedom in the case of offences to which the new rules of the Penal Code apply and the offences to which the provisions before 1 July 2015 apply. The structure of penalties imposed under the current legal regime by common courts should also be examined. Referring to the bill itself, in the opinion of the Polish People's Party in the public interest, it would be necessary to extend the possibility of granting the sentenced person permission to serve a sentence of imprisonment in the electronic surveillance system if a sentence of imprisonment of not more than 2 years was imposed and not, as stated in the draft, 1 year. The risk of such a solution while maintaining the discretion of the judge is negligible. However, this does not prejudice the fact that the Polish People's Party adheres as much to the intentions that were pursued by the designers. We are looking kindly at this project and it can certainly count on our support. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The draft law on the suspension of the sale of agricultural property of the Treasury and on the amendment of certain laws on 8 January 2016 was addressed to public consultation and inter-ministerial arrangements. On 4 March 2016, the bill was submitted by Ms Beata Szydło, President of the Council of Ministers, to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. The main objective of the proposed solutions, as announced in the exposé by the Prime Minister, is to protect Poland from uncontrolled buyout. The existing law on the shaping of the agricultural system and the new law of 5 August 2015 on the shaping of the agricultural system adopted in the previous term of office of the Sejm do not provide such protection. In order to achieve the goal of protecting the Polish land, it is necessary, first of all, to stop the sale of real estate belonging to the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury - I repeat, the land forming part of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury - within 5 years of the date of entry into force of the proposed law. The above solution meets the demands, and I would like to stress very clearly, as reported by farmers, not only Western Pomerania, who want to have a real and equal chance of accessing the country's agricultural land in Poland, and as a result of the constant rise in prices, even gallops or gallops, in terms of the rise in agricultural property prices, they currently do not have enough resources to buy agricultural land. According to the project, the cessation of the sale of the property will not apply to immovable property and parts thereof intended for purposes other than agricultural purposes, in particular for transport investments, housing, sports and recreational facilities, or properties located within the limits of special economic zones, or houses, dwellings, economic buildings and garages, together with the necessary land and residential gardens, and are the last point, agricultural real estate with an area of up to 1 ha. This principle of cessation of sales will not apply to these cases. It should also be pointed out that the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development, at the request of the President of the Agricultural Property Agency, will be able to consent to the sale of property other than those mentioned above, if socio-economic considerations so warrant, e.g. where a farmhouse or other agricultural organisations request a limited invitation to tender for sale to individual farmers. The real protection of Polish land against uncontrolled buyout is also intended to serve the proposed amendments to other laws. The most important amendment concerns the Law of 11 April 2003 on the shaping of the agricultural system. It is assumed, here is an important moment, that the buyers of agricultural property will in principle only be individual farmers, i.e. farmers with agricultural qualifications, who have personally carried out at least 5 years of agricultural holding with an area not exceeding 300 hectares of agricultural area and who live on that holding, and who are fully covered by social insurance for farmers where the area of the holding exceeds 20 hectares of agricultural area. Note - these requirements, I repeat, these statutory requirements will not apply to the acquisition of agricultural property by local government units, the Treasury, but above all to the acquisition by persons close to the seller, i.e. Preliminaries, siblings, siblings, spouse, accompaniers and accompaniers. I draw attention to this information that I have given at the moment, because in a very turbulent way today, we have been accused of doing an attack on our fatherhood. That is not true. [[Voice from the room: True.]] If anyone reads this bill well, I think he will understand in a clear and simple way that this emotion simply does not exist. What does that mean? This means that it will still be possible to transfer agricultural property, e.g. for children, through a donation contract. And one more important thing: agricultural property can also be acquired through inheritance. With the agreement of the President of the Agency by means of an administrative decision against which the appeal will be given to the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development, agricultural property will also be able to acquire other entities providing guarantees of proper agricultural activity and if there is no excessive concentration of agricultural land as a result of the acquisition. The acquisition of agricultural real estate will also be possible for the natural person wishing to establish a family holding, if he is qualified as an agricultural holding, to guarantee the proper pursuit of agricultural activities and to undertake to reside within a period of 5 years from the date of acquisition of the real estate in the municipality in which one of the agricultural real estate is situated, which will be part of the established family holding. In the event of non-acceptance of the acquisition of real estate, the agency will be required to acquire the real estate at the market price at the request of the seller. In order to eliminate the possibility of speculative purchase of agricultural land by persons whose principal activity in life and occupation takes place outside agriculture and their purchase as a form of investment in land, it is necessary to prohibit the disposal or transfer of agricultural property to another entity without the consent of the court for a period of 10 years from the date of acquisition. In order to ensure the expansion and creation of family holdings, as well as to counter the speculative buy-out of agricultural property, it is intended to maintain the existing right of the agency's original buying-in - I also stress this provision, because we are accused of introducing the right of first purchase; I would like to remind you that this law has been in existence for 13 years since 2003, so here we are not making any changes - and the right to acquire agricultural land in the event of a transfer of ownership in a form other than sales. Now move on. The right of first purchase will also be granted to a tenant who is an individual farmer. The right of first purchase and acquisition will not be granted in the cases specified in the Act, among others. where the buyer is an individual farmer resident in the territory of the municipality in which the immovable property is disposed of, or the bordering community, and when the buyer is a close person, so the agency will have no rights here. The new proposed solution is to grant the agency - this is a new solution - the right to buy shares or shares in commercial companies owned by agricultural property. In addition, in the event of a change of partner or the accession of a new partner to a partnership which owns agricultural property, the agency, acting in favour of the Treasury, will be able to make a statement on the acquisition of that property for payment of the monetary equivalent. The proposed provisions aim to prevent the actual acquisition of agricultural property by persons who are not farmers and who have become partners in private companies or who have become shareholders or shareholders in commercial companies owned by agricultural real estate and who actually own these properties by controlling those companies. Another important amendment concerns the Law of 19 October 1991 on the management of agricultural real estate of the State Treasury. It is proposed that agricultural property from the agricultural holding of the Treasury should first be leased out, I stress: leased or sold - it is already in the second place - for the extension or creation of family farms. The Agency will also be able to organise tenders at which the price - which is important, I stress it with all force - will not be a criterion for the selection of the offer, which means that the property will be purchased at a price previously established by the valuer, plus the cost of preparing the property for sale. Detailed regulations will be provided in the relevant regulations of the President of the Agricultural Property Agency. These tenders, and here is an extremely good situation for young farmers, and I would like to make it clear that people under the age of 40 who are not individual farmers will be able to take part, because they do not meet only one condition, the requirement of a 5-year period of personal holding and which will enable the extension of the holding. The purpose of this regulation is to exclude from the acquisition of real estate persons who do not really intend to carry out agricultural activities on the land acquired and who in fact acquired the land under preferential conditions. The aim of this regulation is to prevent the acquisition of real estate by persons who in fact acquire land for the benefit of other persons, with their money, which will ensure that real estate is disposed of to persons who are genuinely interested in running a family farm. The adoption of the draft regulation will lead to a real, not merely declarative, agricultural system based in Poland on family farms, which will be the fulfilment of the standard art. 23 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, according to which the agricultural system in Poland is based on family farms. The year 1997, April, the adoption of this law. Today we are trying to fill this constitutional record. It should be stressed that the draft law prior to the adoption by the Council of Ministers was analysed in detail in terms of its compatibility with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and European Union law. Contrary to some emerging opinions, the draft law does not affect the substance of the property right, because the protection of property is not absolute and can be limited by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. These restrictions may be introduced by law and must, I stress, protect the security of the state or public order or protect the environment, health, public morality or the freedoms and rights of others. The constitutional value of the bill is to ensure the food security of the country. The proper development of the agricultural system should be based on regulation to prevent excessive concentration of private property and to encourage the creation and enlargement of the holdings concerned, as I have just mentioned, in Article. 23 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Agricultural real estate treated as a special value, of a particular nature and purpose should be held in the hands of persons with appropriate qualifications, and the time limit for the disposal of property rights is intended to ensure that this exercise is not a fantasy masking legal turnover contrary to the objectives of the Act. It also requires that the provisions of the Act on the shaping of the agricultural system do not apply to all agricultural property, since the regulations of that law exclude real estate which are intended for purposes other than agricultural purposes in the spatial planning plans. The protection of the rights of owners of agricultural property, in particular as regards the right to dispose of the property, is also served by the solution whereby, in the event that the President of the Agency does not agree to the disposal of such property, the Agency will be required to acquire it at the price resulting from the assessment of that property. In view of the above, it must be concluded that the solutions proposed in the draft law are in line with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Referring directly to European Union legislation, it must be stated that the draft law does not affect the Treaty's freedom of movement of capital between Member States. This is also mentioned in many statements. Nationals of the Member States shall not be treated less favourably on the basis of the solutions proposed in the project than on the date of signature of the Accession Treaty in relation to the situation of citizens of the Republic of Poland. The principle of the movement of capital in parallel to other Treaty freedoms is not absolute. According to the case-law of the Court of Justice concerning the movement of capital, any restriction on the acquisition of immovable property is admissible where it is of public interest, non-discriminatory and meets the requirements of the principle of proportionality. Such restrictions shall be allowed, in so far as they pursue an objective of general interest, to be applied in a non-discriminatory manner and in accordance with the principle of proportionality, i.e. are appropriate and necessary to achieve the objective pursued. The public interest to be protected under the law - including this one - is primarily to ensure the food security of the country, as well as to counter speculation on agricultural land and strengthen the position of family farms, I hope, also to rebuild the very weak portfolio of the Polish farmer. The bill is at least a dozen years late. In the early 1990s, when the Agricultural Property Agency of the Treasury was established - today the heir is the Agricultural Property Agency - there were more than 4700 thousand. ha, which have been taken over mainly on state farms. The agency, which was established at that time, had a basic purpose: to attempt to develop this land and to try to shape the agricultural system of the Republic of Poland under new conditions. This was not quite the case for a number of reasons, for example for one basic reason: debt from former state-owned farms. Not everyone, I stress that it should not be accepted, that I am talking about everyone, not everyone. The priority was to pay it back as soon as possible. I worked at an agency in the years 2000. The payment of this debt was completed in the field office in Olsztyn, if I remember correctly, in 2000, so for 8 years, practically, it was necessary to withdraw this money to repay the debt. High Izbo! This is a document that is officially presented today in plenary of the Sejm. I think this is a good time today to start a substantive discussion here, in this room, and in the agriculture committee. I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz club15 regarding the draft law on the suspension of the sale of the property of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury and on the amendment of certain other laws. The changes proposed in this project are undoubtedly moving in the right direction. We agree with the argument raised in the justification of the project that agricultural land should be regarded as a common good under the protection of public authority. The aim of these changes is also correct, namely to strengthen the protection of agricultural land against speculative buy-out by foreign and national entities which do not guarantee agricultural production in this area. The current rules governing land trading do not effectively protect agricultural land from speculative buy-out. According to the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber concerning the purchase of State agricultural property by foreigners in West Pomeranian, Mazowieckie and Lower Silesian Agricultural Property Agency, from 2011 to 2013 there were reprehensible practices of uncontrolled sale of land owned by the State to foreign entities. He also pointed out in his report on the purchase of agricultural real estate in the various countries of the European Union, the Brussels Trade Institute. He estimated that in Poland, by means of people, so-called. poles, foreign investors purchased over 200,000. ha agricultural land. These situations shouldn't be happening. The land is the good of the whole nation and once sold will not return to Polish hands. It should therefore be given particular concern and put in place such rules as will guarantee the safety of its marketing. The farmer's income is his job on the family farm, not the rotation of the land. Earth is a work tool for a farmer. True farmers do not profit from the sale of agricultural property, only speculators have it, who have purchased land at underpriced prices, often dishonestly and hope that they will be able to sell it with profit. There can be no permission for such disgraceful actions. Although the project is undoubtedly going in the right direction, it unfortunately contains provisions which raise reservations. It should be noted that the proposed changes hit not only foreigners but also Polish farmers. Firstly, the proposed law accepts the maximum area of the family farm - 300 ha. The question arises: Where does the difference between the average size of the agricultural holding, which in 2015 was just over 10 hectares and the size allowed in the project, come from? The basis of the agricultural system in Poland is the family farm, i.e. of the size which the farmer and his family are able to serve themselves. Certainly, a farm of 300 ha is not able to handle one family properly. [[But, sir, the machines are here.]] For rural residents, land is an overriding value, and the lack of adequate rules to protect the interests of family farms can lead to negative consequences such as unequal income opportunities and reduced market competitiveness. In our opinion, the maximum value should be about 10 times the average size of the farm. [[But the regions are different.]] Polish agriculture should focus on the quality of products, not on the size of farms. We are unable to compete with US farms, Western Europe or even Ukraine. Another problem is the banks, which on a broad... [[What a mess.]] ...a scale of loans for investments in agriculture, mainly on the horror of expensive agricultural machinery, and do not share the responsibility for the success of the investment prepared by the farmer. In the event of insolvency of a farmer, banks will be able to bid on holdings and sell them to non-farmers as this project does not protect against such practices. Since we're making strict rules on who can acquire an agricultural property, if we're going to protect the land from getting into the wrong hands, banks can't be in a privileged position, as they are now in the project. The bank is to be a customer partner, in this case the farmer, and be responsible for the success of the credit investment, as is the case in other countries. In Poland, the gap between banks and customers has so far been enormous. In addition, please note that such regulation opens the door to illegal activities. In conclusion, the sale of real estate of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury without statutory restrictions will be considered when the amount of direct payments is aligned with the European level and when farmers' income also reaches a level comparable to that of farmers in Western Europe. Today, the differences regarding the wealth of Polish farmers are huge, and foreigners willing to buy land have many times more money and resources than Poles. At this point we hope that Law and Justice will implement their electoral promises in this area as soon as possible and the level of income of Polish farmers will be significantly higher. Thank you very much.

In relation to companies operating in the countryside, the right to buy back land occurs at the moment of the change of ownership structure, also from father to son. And here as if the children's continued work of the current owner of the company will be threatened. The law therefore limits the right to inherit, including the right to donate and to sit. This last right will be limited within 3 years to individual farmers only. Applicants refer to the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which states that the country's agricultural system is based on a family farm. However, many other constitutional rights that have been violated by this law have not been taken into account, among others. property rights, freedom of economic activity, equality with respect to the law, including the right to equal protection of interests and property rights. Experts stress that, according to the case law of the European Court of Human Rights concerning property rights, the legislator should maintain a reasonable proportion between the measures applied and the objective to be pursued by State action, including those leading to the collection of private property. In limiting the exercise of property rights, the needs of the social interest and the requirements of the individual's rights should be balanced. This jump to agricultural land is treated as a transition, as I said, for manual control of the rotation of land in Poland. The Poles were sure that these times after 1989 were forgotten, it turns out that they did not. This bill is an excessive state interference with private property. Such an approach can ultimately simply lead to investment paralysis in this area of real estate in Poland. The law will further deepen the problem of overly fragmented agrarian structure, and the slow pace of change in this area, as we know, results in a low concentration of forces and opportunities today. The Minister said that such sharpenings exist throughout the Union. The Union is directed towards not concentrating the earth in one hand. Only the Minister forgot to add that the average farm in the European Union is much larger than the average farm in Poland, because Polish farms are among the smallest in the European Union. The average agricultural area of approximately 17 ha is twice the size of the area in the countries of the old Union. This makes it often difficult for Polish farmers to compete with farmers from the European Union, Western Europe. This law, in our opinion, will further worsen the position of Polish agriculture in relation to agriculture in Europe and in the world. As regards the opinion on compliance with Union law, we read that it is to be expected that the proposed legislation will be subject to a thorough analysis by the European Commission of its compliance with Union law. When analysing the proposed restrictions in the context of the case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union, it is expected that they may be challenged by the European Union. Consequently, the Commission may initiate formal infringement proceedings against Poland and, in the absence of such an infringement, bring an action against Poland before the Court. Ladies and gentlemen, there are still many provisions in the bill that can be raised and questioned here. In view of all the comments I mentioned and those that could still be discussed here for a long time, the Modern Club will ask for the rejection of this law at first reading. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u386	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, 2014 Report on the implementation of the National Family Violence Action Programme 2014 This report refers both to activities carried out at central and local level as well as to non-governmental organisations. In 2014, actions were carried out in four areas: prevention and social education, protection and assistance to those affected by family violence, impact on those who use domestic violence, and enhancement of the competences of services and representatives of those involved in the fight against domestic violence. Now I'm going to go over these four areas briefly. I'm moving to area one. Since the problem of family violence is multidimensional, this programme carries out tasks aimed at preventive actions for people and families at risk of violence, measures aimed at protecting people who are violent and those who are violent in the family, but also measures to improve the competences of services and representatives of actors engaged in anti-violence activities. One of the results of the actions undertaken was the cooperation of the various departments involved in the implementation of this programme, but also of representatives and services working in local environments. Preventive programmes, which were among others, have played an important role in their success in the implementation of preventive measures. aimed at children and young people, whose task was and is to create the right lifestyle and appropriate attitudes and behaviour. Within the framework of this programme, among others. The Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy carried out the programme of support for local self-government units in the creation of a system to prevent violence in the family.The Ministry of Education, the programme "Safe and friendly school', the Ministry of Internal Affairs - together safer, and the Ministry of Justice issued informants, brochures, led the contact database of those who coordinated the tasks, as well as those who ran aid centres for victims. On the other hand, the Ministry of Health, through the Polish Agency for the Solution of Alcohol Problems, carried out information activities, training for services and conducted a nationwide hotline for people experiencing violence in the family, the Blue Line. A major element of this area is also the change of public awareness. This change in public awareness takes place through campaigns. These social campaigns, which were aimed at, among others, the decline in violent behaviour, as well as the change in perception of the problems of violence not only as an individual case but as a broader phenomenon to be addressed. Regarding prevention, consideration should also be given to cooperation between local authorities and the Churches in order to introduce elements of education on the phenomenon of violence in the field of church counseling, religious unions or pre-marriage programmes. More than a thousand initiatives have also been undertaken in this cooperation. They were conducted both at the level of municipalities and powiats. When discussing these activities, it is also worth mentioning the obligation imposed on local authorities to develop and implement a municipal, district and voivodeship programme against family violence and to protect victims of domestic violence. More than 2 000 in 2014 The municipalities, almost 300 powiats and all local governments of the voivodships have developed such programs. In describing the area of prevention, it is also important to mention the studies carried out on the Diagnosis and comparison of the scale of family violence and the effectiveness of measures taken to combat family violence. These studies show that, in particular, I wanted to point out that the main cause of violence is the problem with alcohol. In the second place there is a lack of money, then there are marital problems, but what came out especially in these studies, is that often nothing affected family violence. Yes more than 17% respondents replied. Details of the report will be found on the website of the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy. The second area of action under the national programme is the protection and assistance of those affected by family violence. Throughout the country, according to this law, the people affected by violence in 2014 were 197 989. The aid was provided in the form of counselling 156 082 persons, including medical counseling 6328 persons, psychological - 63 953 persons, legal - 41 500 persons, social - 86 186 persons, professional and family - 25 267 persons. It should be noted that in 2014 it operated in municipalities of more than 2 000. interdisciplinary teams and 70,000. working groups dealing with individual cases. There were 905 units throughout Poland, including 690 institutions run by municipalities and 215 institutions run by powiats. Among the institutions, the vast majority were consultative points and crisis intervention centres, as well as specialised support centres for victims of domestic violence. The smallest number of institutions in Poland are support centres and homes for mothers with minors and pregnant women. When discussing aid institutions, it is worth pointing out the existence of 35 specialised support centres in the country for victims of domestic violence. The operation of these centres is a task entrusted to the county authorities and the funds are transferred from the State budget. The cost of maintaining specialised support centres for victims of violence in 2014 was over 12 million. In these specialised centres, people who experience violence receive free 24/7 assistance. More than 7717 advice was provided in these centres in 2014, including 5344 women, 1112 men and 1261 children. They included older people, 375 people, and 406 people with disabilities. Another form of support directed to people experiencing violence was nationwide and local round-the-clock phones of trust. In 2014, there were three nationwide calls of trust, while the number of local phones of trust was 467 throughout the country, 116 of which were 24 hours a day and 351 functioned at certain times. Another area is the area of impact on violent people. The application of the Blue Card procedure should be taken into account within this area. In 2014, more than 99 000 were launched. blue card procedures. Most of the procedures were initiated by the police, because more than 77 thousand, then by representatives of social services. There has also been a significant increase in the initiation of the procedure by educational representatives - more than 6 thousand, while the least started procedures were still the blue cards by the representatives of the municipal committees to solve alcohol problems, because 694, and health representatives. Like I said, more than 99,000 were founded. The Blue Cards in 2014 and the number of cases completed as a result of lack of legitimacy is 14 851 and the number of cases completed as a result of family violence is 41 522. Priority action aimed at those who use violence in the family is the implementation of programmes for correction and education in the conditions of freedom and penal units. In 2014, the corrective and educational impact of violence was over 6 000. Whereas the actual number of people who joined the programmes is more than 8 000. Another action aimed at those who use violence in the family is the implementation of psychological and therapeutic programmes for those who use violence. This is a new task in the national programme. It is also extremely important to provide protection and security for those affected by violence. This purpose is served by means of the measures provided for in the criminal procedure and in material criminal law to isolate the perpetrators of violence in cases where this is necessary to protect the life or health of victims. One example of effective forms of isolation is a precautionary measure by ordering the accused to commit a crime of violence against a person who lives together to leave the premises occupied together with the victim, where there is a legitimate concern that the accused will again commit a crime of violence against that person, especially when he threatened to commit such a crime. This measure shall apply for a period of not more than 3 months. If the grounds for its application have not ceased, the court of first instance having jurisdiction to hear the case at the request of the prosecutor may extend its application for further periods of no longer than 3 months. In 2014, the number of suspects against whom the above mentioned list was applied. the preventive measure in the form of an order to leave the dwelling occupied together with the victim was more than 2,300 persons, for example. In 2011, there were 849 such cases. The last area of action under the national programme is to increase the competences of services and representatives of actors carrying out anti-violence tasks in the family. In 2014, guidelines were developed for training against family violence for 2014 and 2015. The Marshals of the voivodships organized trainings for people carrying out tasks related to the prevention of violence in the family. In 2014, 114 such trainings took place. In total, more than 3,000 were trained. employees of first contact. The Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy organised a conference in this area. The National Family Violence Prevention Programme 2014 and 2020 - the present and the future. Finally, it should be pointed out that the actions I am discussing are only a summary and closing part of 2014. At the same time, it should be pointed out that the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy sees the need for legislative amendments to the anti-violence law, and this work is currently ongoing in the Ministry. There is a need to re-bureaucratise and simplify the procedure of Blue Cards, but also to eliminate stigmatisation by this card. Following the drafting of the bill, it will be submitted to a wide range of consultations. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo.]]
