The report covers 2014 and was prepared by the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy in cooperation with other ministries and local government units and NGOs. The document was entered in the Sejm on 15 October 2015, in print No 178. On 13 January, he was assigned to work in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. The report on the implementation of the programme was adopted on 24 February by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs, which recommended that the High House adopt the report on printing No 268. The programme has a long-term perspective. It sets out areas, directions and types of action aimed at promoting prevention and carrying out tasks towards those who are both perpetrators and victims of domestic violence at all levels of public administration, in cooperation with actors representing local environments. The National Programme against Violence in the Family has been implemented in four areas: prevention and social education, protection and assistance to those affected by family violence, impact on those who are violent in the family, as well as in the last area: improving the competences of services and representatives of those involved in anti-violence activities in the family. In the first area: prevention and social education have been researched on the level of violence among Poles. The report presents the results of studies on the sources and percentage of the phenomenon by type of violence used. In this area, nationwide and local social campaigns were conducted. Cooperation was also undertaken between local authorities and churches and religious unions located in the area. These activities covered more than 1 000. initiatives in more than 1 000 municipalities and 50 powiats. Coverage and educational programmes have also been developed for family violence prevention activities, particularly in relation to children, women and the elderly or with disabilities. The Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy is implementing a cover programme to support local self-government units in the creation of a system against domestic violence. About PLN 3 million was allocated to this programme in 2014. The programme against violence and the protection of its victims has been developed and implemented at all levels of the local government in more than 2 000. Municipalities, nearly 300 powiats and regional governments. The second area concerns actions aimed at protecting and helping those affected by violence. In this area, interdisciplinary teams and working groups have been operational. In 2014, the municipalities operated more than 2.5 thousand. Teams and 70,000. working groups dealing with individual cases. The next action within this area is the creation of support facilities for people affected by violence - in Poland there are about 1 000. such facilities - and increase the availability of national and local phones of trust. In 2014, a total of over 60 thousand were held. talks, which resulted in 16 thousand talks. intervention. In more than 1350 cases, this intervention concerned the isolation of children from the environment in which they experience violence. The third area concerns the impact on violent people. Under the Blue Card procedure, in 2014 proceedings were initiated in nearly 100,000. cases, including most cases, have been initiated by the police and social assistance units. In addition, corrective and educational programmes were developed and implemented for violent people, with over 6 000 participating in 2014. people - and psychological and therapeutic programs. Since 2017, this task will be financed from the State budget in accordance with the programme schedule. In 2014, more than 1.6 thousand joined the programme at local government level. persons. The fourth area, the last one, is to improve the competences of the services and representatives of the actors involved in anti-violence activities. The programme has developed and organised a training system for those carrying out anti-violence tasks in the family. In 2014, more than 3,000 were trained in total. persons. A total of 16 400 000 are planned for the 2014 budget to carry out family-based anti-violence tasks. PLN, of which in the voivodship budgets more than PLN 14 million and in the special reserve more than 1800 thousand. In the final plan, at 31 December, this amount in the budget for the implementation of the programme after the change amounted to almost 16 700 000. zł. These funds were used in more than 98%. The activities of the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy in the implementation of the programme are supported by the Monitoring Team. Against Violence in the Family. The team shall participate in the development of guidelines for the organisation of trainings and shall evaluate the proposals of local authorities under the cover programme financed by the Minister. In addition, it initiates legislative action in the area of anti-violence in the family and gives an opinion on the areas of research and nationwide social campaigns to which the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy is obliged to implement. The National Action Programme against Violence in the Family, which complements the Anti-violence Act in an operational manner, is part of the implementation of state policy in this area. Despite the large number of recommendations resulting from the analysis of the report which is necessary to improve and improve the implementation of the programme, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the adoption of the report on the implementation of the government programme by the High House. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u391	Thank you. Mrs Marzena Okła-Drewnowicz, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u392	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the implementation of the National Family Violence Action Programme for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2014. We know that family violence is one of the worst social pathologies. We also know that the history of violence actually goes back centuries. In Poland, domestic violence has been said out loud for several years, perhaps only 20 years. Once upon a time, we didn't even have the opportunity to speak openly about domestic violence. Victims of domestic violence could not even dream of using such forms of help as they are today. Over the last few years, I think, we have made tremendous progress in this area. However, this does not mean that all possibilities and aid have already been exhausted, on the contrary, there is still much to be done. Usually, victims don't report to the police for any reason. Very often these are individual reasons, because the perpetrator is e.g. The only breadwinner in the family, he's the one. the owner of the apartment. It also often continues to repent of the shame that violence in the family is, however, a form of social pathology that cannot be said about. Therefore, it is very important to help victims of violence not only those instruments addressed directly to them, so this psychological assistance or legal assistance, but also social campaigns. I think that this report shows in such a detailed way what forms of assistance are today and which ones are, so to speak, the most effective. But I would also like to remind you, I think, of two very important pieces of legislation that have been put into effect over the past few years. First of all, I would like to talk about the law, about the first law of 2005, i.e. the law on anti-violence in the family. Surely this was a breakthrough, a breakthrough in showing that violence in the family is a bad thing to stigmatize and that it is absolutely necessary to help the victims. I think that the second very important legal act is the amendment of this law of 2010, which introduced the need to develop this National Anti-Mounting Programme in the Family. And today, as the Minister said, this programme contains a description of the activities carried out in four main areas, namely prevention and social education, protection and assistance to those affected by family violence, impact on those who are violent in the family and improving the competences of the services and representatives of those involved in anti-violence activities in the family. I would like to draw attention to the studies that were commissioned and carried out by the Ministry of Labour and Social Policy in 2014. In 2014, fewer respondents declared that they had experienced domestic violence than in 2010. Fewer perpetrators were admitted to domestic violence than in 2010. There were also fewer witnesses of domestic violence. According to the victims' statements, violence was most commonly used against women. - over 67%. According to reports from victims and witnesses, the most common victims of violence in the family were men - over 76%. In 2014 26% The respondents declared that they knew families in their environment where there was violence against the elderly or the disabled. Nearly 20% of all respondents declared childhood experience of at least one form of violence. Statistical data from the implementation of this programme for 2014 show that a total of 1288 local diagnoses have been developed in Poland. These are very important diagnoses, because they concern both municipal, district and voivodship levels. About... I would also like to refer to this change of social awareness, because, as I remember from my own work in the late 1990s, violence was a rather hidden phenomenon, and today we see the huge impact of all the social campaigns that were of great importance, on the change of social consciousness. And so, for example, in the analysis of statistics, over 99 000 used the Blue Card procedure in 2014. Victims. Compared to 2013, this is a huge difference because in 2013 the blue cards were filled with 70,000. Next, I would like to emphasise the importance of interdisciplinary teams, which build an interdisciplinary system in which employees of various services and institutions are involved. Research has shown that over these seven years, violence has fallen by 10%. I also think that it is important to stress the fact that there has been a decrease in the use, so to speak, of people who have suffered violence in the family from institutions. I think that the reduction in the number of people using these facilities has also had a significant impact on the code changes introduced in 2010 by amending the law on anti-violence in the family, which makes it possible to issue an order to leave the premises by the perpetrator of violence in the family and to prohibit contact with the person experiencing violence. This is the first time we introduced this in the amendment of the law in 2010. This means that this measure is used and that the perpetrator leaves the house, not the victim, often a woman with her children. Yes, we see that this measure is not yet fully used, but we are aware that it is a process that requires training and time. We see great progress in this matter. The important thing is that such a provision is that such a provision works, because this is what we, legislators, people who work on it, who help in various institutions, e.g. In social assistance centers, it is the perpetrator who leaves the place of residence, not the victim, often a woman with children. We have also introduced, and this is what the report is talking about, programmes of correctional and educational influences for those who use violence in the family. Over the years, these programmes have increased their potential and, thanks to their implementation, more than 8 000 have been affected in 2014. people who are violent in the family. It's a lot, for example. In 2007, the number of people benefiting from such a scheme was just 900. I think that the most important thing is - even on the basis of these studies that I quoted from 2014 - that there is a reduction in the scale of the phenomenon of violence in the family. The first studies, in 2007, showed that 36% Poles experienced any violence. The 2010 study's result is a decrease of 33%. 2014 shows that more than 24% respondents experienced violence. So this is a very important decrease in the number of people affected by domestic violence. Most often this violence is psychological violence, including physical violence, further sexual violence and economic violence. This report clearly shows that the forms of aid proposed in the existing anti-violence law are well implemented because, on the one hand, the scale of the phenomenon of violence is decreasing, and on the other hand, we see that many instruments are increasingly being used more and more. Courts. So I think that on the one hand the phenomenon is smaller, and on the other hand the effectiveness is the most important here. I think there's a lot left. This programme has to be assessed well or even very well in 2014, and this does not mean that no further monitoring is needed. On the contrary - monitor, improve so that victims have the best help from different institutions, but also to surround the perpetrators with some corrective programs, as it has been used so far to actually solve these problems, because children are often directly and indirectly victims. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u393	Thank you very much. Ms Agnieszka Chasing, Kukiz,15 has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-03-10-sejm-13-2.u394	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Parliamentary Club15 on the implementation of the 2014-2020 National Anti-Mounting Programme for the Family in 2014. The report itself is a very extensive study that actually exposes a big elephant on clay legs. Elephant is a system of support by the state of families experiencing a crisis, an inefficient, inefficient, bureaucratic system of social assistance, which in this case was built up by various other institutions and offices, which are supposed to help the Polish family - in a crisis unfortunately. Family violence itself, of course, is a huge crisis, and that's what we have to call it, it often poses a threat to life, to health, and it's the result of complex causes, and there's no doubt about it. It is good that we are solving this problem, that we are monitoring this phenomenon, that we are trying to help such victims or the perpetrators themselves. I told you about the elephant and his clay legs, because in this report, you can see that he's about to fall. For years in Poland, such a messy web has been constructed, which is supposed to help the Polish family in the crisis. The effect is that 70% families who benefit from social assistance, never get out of this web, even by reference to the inheritance of learned helplessness, yet generations of these families unfortunately enter this system, even without their own will. Imagine a family that has problems with violence, these problems are often complex, with unemployment, with educational failure, with disabilities or addictions, which were very often seen here in the report, are linked to violence. Let's imagine, ladies and gentlemen, that such a family is a customer of at least 11 - at least, and sometimes that over - institutions: social assistance, court, family assistant, PFRON, employment office, curator, therapists, police, counsellors, etc., that's a whole lot of it, often NGOs. In each of these institutions, as the family goes there, there is a separate diagnosis, there is a separate action, several specialists, I will say colloquially, dig in my own way and according to my procedures in this family. Of course, there are huge resources behind this, there are a lot of procedures, there is a whole staff of officials. The effect is most often this, it can also be seen in this report, that each of these specialists, to whom the family goes, says that he can't do anything here, because a plot is in another specialist, and according to the law he did his job, and now you have to direct the family to another institution. And that's how it goes, this family goes around these institutions very often for a few years, even getting used to this system, living in a certain symbiosis. Just what? You just can't see any effects, and she's still stuck with her problems. In this report, just like domestic violence, that's exactly what you see. First, the state created various institutions, obliged them to help such a family, a family with violence, gave each of them a separate procedure, separate tools - Police, social assistance, guardians to the court, assistants, alcoholic committees, which also participate in this - then saw, realized that all these institutions were not effective, because they simply did not cooperate with each other, and decided to lead them to cooperate with each other. The report shows that it is followed by staff, training, various instruments to force cooperation. Of course, it's a good thing that you realized and started to control it, but we have such an effect that very often in this family with violence many people are interfering with it, very many specialists. And all those people who have had to deal with it - and I am a person who has worked with victims of violence for many years - can say boldly that all those people who are trying to interfere there can actually do harm. Everyone who has to deal with violence knows that experimenting with their family is very easy to do about the effect of re-injuring the family for years to withdraw from the fact that it has even reached for help. I know that we are talking about the report now, but I would like to encourage you, I would like to encourage you to change your thinking about helping the Polish family in the crisis, thinking about looking at the family as a whole. Let us finally look at it by working with it and that it is a closed system. Let us help her to see her own weaknesses, but also to see her strengths and resources. When I say "help," I mean, of course, outside help, animator help. It may be an assistant, but he's an animator. The animator is not the person who tells the family what to do to get out of the crisis, and gives her his prescriptions according to his procedures, but the animator is the person who skillfully directs this family so that she finds her power, her resources and tries to get out of the situation in which she is in. Then the family can diagnose its situation, and violence is also a situation that it will be able to diagnose. Of course, this animator is the person closest to him, the only person who will be able to contact other services, along with this family, plan the form of help, plan what tools are needed for this family to get out of this situation, and, of course, will also plan the money that this family will use for it. This system of work is called a system of working with the family. This is not a novelty, such a system is successfully applied in other countries of the European Union. For many years, in recent years, it has been well, with the pilot effect used in innovative projects from the European Union, also in Poland. The advantages of this model are above all that we involve the family in a plan for helping her, which at the same time creates the conditions for her to feel responsible for changing her situation and realizes it in the way we work with her, it is also precisely targeted assistance according to the needs of this family, and not according to what exactly the state has for this family, the re-bureaucratisation of social assistance, and at the same time focusing on actual work with the family. Family crisis needs to be helped, there is no doubt here, but the current system only consolidates this crisis and costs huge money. The government's appeal to undertake even in this respect a thorough reform of this clay elephant and proposed such a system, tried to lean on a system that will be effective, because the Polish family can be helped effectively, and especially this family in crisis. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This law gives the possibility to establish other forms of care for the youngest than existing forms, such as children's club or day care provider. The provisions which entered into force on 1 October 2011 also allow for the legal employment of a nanny. The Act sets out the principles of the various forms of childcare, the conditions of the services provided, the number and qualifications of caretakers, the supervision of the conditions and the quality of such care. Art. The 6th Act requires the Council of Ministers to submit an annual report on its implementation to the Sejm and the Senate by 30 September. The present document summarises the effects of its operation in 2014 and is the third evaluation of the functioning of the Act. In 2014, as in previous years, the Act had some effect. Small children were being cared for at a rate similar to that of the previous year. The number of such institutions in 2014 was 2,5 000. In 2014, care institutions offered a total of just over 71 thousand. Places. Every 16th child in Poland under the age of 3 had guaranteed care in nursery, children's club or day care provider. This is a modest result, but the data on the village is even worse - approx. 4700 seats were located in rural areas, i.e. only 6,5% all the places. Dramaticly, it appears that only 1% children with disabilities are taken care of. In addition, significant geographical differences can be observed here. As part of the Operational Programme, 239 contracts have been completed. On the basis of them, only 380 childcare institutions started their activities. Injustice mainly concerns individuals who carry out these forms of childcare. The municipality is obliged to finance the stay of the child only if such a unit, i.e. the form of support, leads just the municipality itself or the local government, and maybe - and this is a key word: it can - finance the stay of the child if the institution is run by another entity, e.g. a private entity or a non-governmental organisation. Institutions run by other entities, such as organisations or individuals, have the right to the same rules as local authorities. Very often they provide services at a really high level. The rule that should apply to financing is a rule that is based on the quality of services, and here it cannot be clearly stated that only institutions run by the local government provide a good offer. Parents have the right to choose and this market allows for this verification. This concerns the development of an electronic system enabling applications to be submitted in the perpetual accounting procedure via the electronic system. The aim is to facilitate the work of courts and to speed up proceedings in such cases. Although work on the system has been completed, the system tests have not been completed. Too short time is indicated for thorough preparation for the use of the system. As the proposals are formalised, it seems advisable to complete the necessary training and training. Given the circumstances indicated, the project is well-founded. By acting on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club will support further legislative work on this project. [[Applause]]
